#5056. Where Does Christine Baranski Live? Exploring the Gilded Age Star’s Real Estate Portfolio

Her Gilded Age character lives in a grand Upper East Side brownstone, but where does Christine Baranski live? The Emmy- and Tony–winning actor was born in Buffalo, New York, and still owns property in her home state in the form of a Manhattan pied-à-terre—and yes, like Agnes van Rhijn’s onscreen dwelling, it is on the Upper East Side. However, the Good Wife star primarily lives in Connecticut, where her late husband grew up and where the couple raised their two daughters, Isabel and Lily.

Read on to learn more about Baranski’s property portfolio.

For decades, Baranski has lived in Connecticut’s Litchfield County. Her first visit to the region was on a 1982 motorcycle ride; her future husband, fellow actor Matthew Cowles, was the driver. Cowles grew up in an 18th-century farmhouse in the small town of Bethlehem. “I had such a feeling of destiny about it,” Baranski recalled in a 2008 interview, “I loved it. I loved the smells in the air, the farms, the field. It was a dream. When Matthew took me into his home, I thought, ‘We’re gonna raise children here.’” The couple married the next year, briefly residing in an NYC apartment before returning to raise their daughters, born in 1984 and in 1987, in Cowles’s childhood home. “I had such a sense of eternity, like I was meant to be there,” she added.

A 2003 interview reported that Baranski primarily lived in Bethlehem and maintained a lake house about half an hour away, and it seems that this is still the case. “I have a marvelous house on a lake in Connecticut and I’ve hosted some very famous actors there,” the actor said in a recent interview with The Guardian, adding that those famed visitors have, on occasion, stripped naked for a swim. “It has a very long dock that has been ‘grandfathered’ in, as in they don’t allow a dock that long to be built anymore, but this is about 100 years old. You sit in the living room in summer and watch the sun set, and then go out into the evening and skinny dip in the lake.”

Cowles died in 2014, but Baranski remains deeply rooted in his hometown. The Mamma Mia! star is so entrenched in the community that she became active in the Housatonic Valley Association, a local environmental nonprofit, and currently serves as one of its directors. “I enjoy so much the beauty of Litchfield County,” Baranski said in 2009. “I couldn't imagine living in a more beautiful place.”

In 2004, Baranski bought a pied-à-terre on Manhattan’s Upper East Side for $1.27 million. Located in a 1920s structure where Jackie Kennedy lived as a child, the two-bedroom, two-bathroom apartment featured a 21-foot-long living room with hardwood floors, a wood-burning fireplace flanked by built-in bookshelves, and yellow-striped wallpaper. A galley kitchen opened to a small dining room painted a soft sage green and decorated with elegant paintings in ornate gold frames, while the bedrooms were done up in distinct palettes: one was blue and white, while the other was tan with pops of red and orange. Both had their own en suite bath.

“Happily, now after years of commuting to LA and trying to do the whole bicoastal thing, The Good Wife shoots in Greenpoint, Brooklyn,” Baranski said in a 2011 New York Times article. “Our kids are grown up and in graduate school, so I have this life where I can enjoy the city and not worry about racing home to Connecticut.” The actor shared her typical Sunday routine in the city, which included reading in a café, visiting museums, and seeing friends. “I tend to call Sundays a girlfriend day because my husband is in Connecticut, so more often than not I’m alone here, which is a chance to catch up with girlfriends,” she said. “I might do a lunch at one of the hole-in-the-wall French bistros in the neighborhood, but sometimes I skip lunch in favor of an early dinner.” Then, the actor turns in early. “If I’m in town on a Sunday, it usually means I’m working Monday, and often I’m the first shot of the day, which means you’re up at 4:30. That means going to bed early. So I’ll go to my early dinner and then try to get myself to sleep by 9 or 9:30.”

Inhabitants of the co-op building, designed by architect Lafayette A. Goldstone, have access to a backyard garden, a gym, and a laundry room. Baranski listed her unit in the nine-story building in February 2021 for $1.39 million, and it sold that June.

Baranski didn’t move too far, however. In December 2020, the Good Fight star upgraded to a larger $2.2 million duplex unit in the same building. Listing photos showed that the home was sold with a few spaces under construction, so she likely added some custom touches before moving in. The apartment has four bedrooms, three bathrooms, and a much larger eat-in kitchen than the one found in Baranski’s previous unit. It still boasts a wood-burning fireplace in the living room, accessed from the foyer through an arched doorway. On its other end, the living room opens to a spacious formal dining room. A guest bedroom rounds out the lower level. The remaining bedrooms are at the top of a winding staircase. Another fireplace, tucked between built-ins, graces one bedroom, which shares a Jack-and-Jill bathroom with another bedroom—which could be used as a dressing room—that boasts six closets. The final bedroom has a full en suite bathroom.

#5054. ZachXBT Names French Bank Crédit Mutuel Arkéa as Alleged Victim of $133M Crypto Theft

ZachXBT Names French Bank Crédit Mutuel Arkéa as Alleged Victim of $133M Crypto Theft

Onchain investigator ZachXBT has named French banking group Crédit Mutuel Arkéa as the alleged victim of a crypto theft of more than $133 million.

"Disclosing the EU financial institution exploited for $133M+ is allegedly Credit Mutuel Arkea, a French bank with $235B in assets," ZachXBT wrote on his Telegram channel on Tuesday. "The attack on the bank occurred for multiple days. I suspect the amount stolen is larger when accounting for OTC activity."

Crédit Mutuel Arkéa has not publicly confirmed any theft. Decrypt has reached out to the bank for comment.

The mutual banking group has been preparing to move into crypto. In May, French financial newspaper L'Agefi reported that it had applied for a license under MiCA, the EU's crypto rulebook, to offer crypto services to its retail customers and to provide them as a white-label service through its Arkéa Banking Services division.

In an update earlier on Tuesday, he said he had identified a further $27 million in exchange withdrawals by the attacker, bringing the total to more than $133 million, and that some of those withdrawals took place after his initial disclosure. He also published four Ethereum addresses he said were linked to the theft.

"My working theory is a large financial institution in Western Europe was exploited and it's currently being covered up," ZachXBT wrote at the time, accusing it of not having conducted "proper incident disclosure" and thereby preventing law enforcement from freezing some $30 million in stolen funds.

He did not say how he had identified the bank, or how the attacker gained access to its exchange accounts.

The allegation comes days after the $6.3 billion hack of Binance, the largest crypto theft on record, which the FBI has attributed to North Korean hackers. ZachXBT said the attacker in the suspected EU theft remains unidentified, but that he could say "with a high degree of confidence it is NOT Lazarus Group," the North Korean hacking collective, "based on multiple laundering indicators."

#5052. Democrats claim control of the US Senate

The shift in Senate control could change the path for the CLARITY Act, reshape committee leadership and bring closer scrutiny of Trump’s crypto businesses.

Democrats have won control of the US Senate after flipping Republican-held seats in Texas, Ohio, North Carolina, South Carolina, Kansas and Iowa, according to the Associated Press.

The majority was secured after Democrat Senator Josh Turek won the Senate race for Iowa, bringing Democrats and independents who caucus with them to 51 seats.

The result reshapes the outlook for the stalled CLARITY Act and determines which lawmakers lead committees overseeing the industry.

Democratic control could also bring more congressional scrutiny of Trump-linked crypto businesses and regulators’ decisions. Control of the Senate could also affect confirmation of future presidential nominees to regulatory posts.

“It’s too early to say exactly what this means for crypto, but one thing is clear: nothing will pass without cross-partisan support,” Jerome de Tychey, president of Ethereum France and co-founder of EthCC, told Cointelegraph.

“The CLARITY Act failed its Senate procedural vote 49 to 50 in September, and if it isn’t passed by January, the next Congress starts again from scratch. So the industry will have to go back to the table and make the case for blockchain again, on substance.”

De Tychey said some Democratic demands could help strengthen the industry’s regulatory framework.

“Conflict-of-interest rules and customer protection obligations are things a mature industry should welcome. In Europe, we already live with them under MiCA,” he said, referring to the European Union’s Markets in Crypto-Assets regulation.

However, he warned against allowing crypto policy to become entrenched along party lines.

“The biggest risk for crypto in America is for it to become entrenched as a partisan issue; that could set the US back several years.”

Fred Hsu, co-founder and CEO of D3, a core contributor to Doma Protocol, similarly said durable regulation would require support from both parties.

“The midterms are a good reminder that the industry can’t rely on one party to deliver them, and should be making its case to lawmakers on both sides now,” Hsu said in comments provided ahead of the election.

Related: 1inch lawyer sees agencies driving US crypto regulation as midterms unfold

#5050. 10 Best Bedside Lamps & Table Lamps (2026)

Before you spin into a redecorating frenzy (step away from the chandeliers…), consider the transformative power of the table lamp. The best bedside lamps are a necessary lighting addition to any bedroom—nighttime reader or not. They also have staying power away from the bedside, should they find their home in another part of the house as your taste shifts: add them to a shelf on a bookcase, show them off in an entryway, let one adorn your kitchen countertop.

Accent lighting has always been a thing, but lately, with the rise of anti–Big Light content on TikTok and Reels, the demand for cute, trendy ambient lighting options is going way up. And we get it—relying on built-in overhead light fixtures feels cold and corporate, while smaller lamps create a warmer ambience. That’s why we had the bright idea to test a curated selection of top picks from some of our favorite brands (in addition to other great options that score high design marks in our books). In each of our reviews you’ll find an AD editor’s testimony as to what makes each light stand out, how they use it in their space, and what’s something buyers should keep in mind. The hardest part now will be deciding on just one (or two).

While you can use a higher watt light bulb than your light fixture suggests, we wouldn’t recommend it. Wattage relates to how much power a particular device uses, and if more than the design allows is being used, you face the risk of ‘overlamping,’ meaning your fixture will start to run particularly hot. This is bad for a few reasons: Overheating can melt bulb sockets and wiring and, in the worst cases, lead to an electrical fire. It may also cost you more in the long run and be less energy-efficient—if your bulbs are drawing too much energy, it’ll likely mean having to replace them more frequently. If you’re still confused on the correct bulb to look for, we hope this handy visual guide will help.

Did the instruction manual for assembly look like it could have been ripped out of an astrophysics textbook? We wanted to let you know exactly what to expect from your purchases and called out anything surprising. We also double-checked if a brand provided you with a complimentary bulb or if it’s something you should add to your next Amazon order.

Sizing: While we provide specs on exact measurements in all these reviews, sizing can still be a difficult factor to gauge. To help you determine whether or not that CB2 lamp will totally overwhelm your nightstand as well as help you compare the lamps of this list, we shot each close-up with a 2.4-ounce candle as a base point for size comparison.

Our reviews were designed to give insight on why each lamp could be the right lamp for your Scandi oasis or midcentury abode. #5049. Ethereum dev targets 2028 for quantum resistance, a year early

Ethereum researcher Justin Drake said it is their “hope” that Ethereum will achieve its post-quantum and post-AI transition by December 2028.

Ethereum Foundation researcher Justin Drake is targeting December 2028 to complete Ethereum’s post-quantum and post-AI transition, a year earlier than the foundation’s previously announced quantum-resistance deadline.

Speaking at Devcon 8 in Mumbai on Tuesday, Drake outlined the accelerated timeline. “Our hope is that by December 2028 we’ll be able to wrap up the full post-quantum and post-AI transition,” Drake said, adding that developers have 24 months to deliver three additional network upgrades.

The Ethereum Foundation’s September roadmap established December 2029 as its deadline for achieving quantum resistance across Ethereum’s execution, consensus and data layers.

Drake’s remarks signal a more ambitious timeline but do not formally replace the foundation’s existing roadmap, which is scheduled for reassessment in January 2027.

In August, Drake outlined plans for production-ready leanVM technology in 2027, followed by post-quantum deployments across Ethereum’s three layers in 2028.

The timeline has drawn scrutiny from other blockchain developers. In October, Starknet’s community backed research into becoming an independent post-quantum layer-1 network.

StarkWare CEO Eli Ben-Sasson previously told Cointelegraph that Ethereum’s 2029 target was “too late,” warning that deadlines could slip.

#5046. Midterms Blue Wave Makes $917K for One Polymarket Trader, If Alaska Doesn’t Flip

Midterms Blue Wave Makes $917K for One Polymarket Trader, If Alaska Doesn’t Flip

A "blue wave” swept the U.S. midterms as the Democrats took control of both the House of Representatives and the Senate, in a crushing blow to President Donald Trump.

Polymarket predictor 0xA2 is the platform's most profitable trader over the past 24 hours, according to the site's daily leaderboard. They are currently $917,000 in profit, although most of that is still in an active position that has a real (ableit small) chance of upset.

The user wagered $609,644 on the Republicans not winning any Senate swing states when odds sat at just 36%, a market which has since surged to 87%. This turned his stake in to $1.48 million, putting him $860,000 in the green.

Its important to remember: these are unrealized gains and could drop at any moment as 0xA2 waits for Alaska's final verdict. Polymarket traders put a 14% chance on an upset—and while this may seem small, the site has seen similarly sized upsets recently—as in the case of Brazil's presidential election two weeks ago.

0xA2 also profited $106,115 by predicting that the Democrats would win the Texas Senate—profits that have been realized. Oddly, the user's second largest active position is $1.42 million on the U.S. not confirming that aliens exist before 2027.

"Election night showed how quickly round-the-clock markets move when traditional exchanges are closed. As polls began closing at 6pm ET, Kalshi’s odds of a Democratic sweep went from about 74% to 99%," Kelly Ye, Head of Research at digital asset management firm Talos, said in a note shared with Decrypt. "Prediction-market trading peaked between 8pm and 10pm ET, led by a small number of large trades."

It was a big day for Polymarket users profiting, with the top 10 users on the platform profiting more than $170,000, per the leaderboard. To put that into perspective, on a fairly random day in March this year, the top daily winner on Polymarket profited $154,000, according to the WayBackMachine, and the 10th biggest winner that day gained $29,700.

The midterms attracted major activity on leading prediction markets Polymarket and Kalshi. According to Precition.com data, midterm markets attracted more than $546 million in notional volume over the past two weeks.

"The 2024 election was the breakthrough moment for prediction markets. It was their first major real-world test and the event that really drove volume and open interest," Dash, the pseudonymous data lead at Prediction.com, told Decrypt. "Today, sports dominate trading volume, but Politics is still the key driving factor for open interest."

During the 2024 presidential race, Polymarket became what Bernstein analysts called the world’s largest election prediction market, with its flagship market topping $3.6 billion in volume. Maybe more importantly, predictors favored Trump for most of the campaign, while traditional polls kept him neck and neck with Kamala Harris. Prediction market users took this as a sign that prediction markets are more accurate than traditional political polling.

"Increasingly, people are looking for accurate forecasts on election results," Dash said, "and prediction markets are one place they can be found."

Those forecasts aren't always accurate, though. Just two weeks ago, Polymarket predictors whiffed Brazil's presidential election, with users giving Luiz Inácio Lula da Silva about a 20% chance of winning ahead of election day, before he ultimately beat Flávio Bolsonaro to take a fourth term.

Experts told Decrypt at the time that Brazil’s prediction market ban had left the odds in the hands of traders with little local knowledge to correct the odds. Plus, experts warned that a 20% chance of something happening is still a very real chance.

"Ultimately, something that has an 80% chance of happening does not happen 20% of the time," Luke Nolan, senior researcher at CoinShares, said.

So that 14% chance that 0xA2 loses his $860,000 in profit should be taken seriously.

#5045. Quince’s $190 Quilt Set Is Worth Every Dollar

Since its 2018 founding, Quince’s enormous catalog and affordable prices have captured national attention, leading many a shopper to wonder whether the brand was too good to be true. The shopping team at AD set out for an answer by putting Quince’s full lineup to the test. Ultimately, we found 17 favorite items.

The direct-to-consumer retailer’s bamboo sheets are among our favorite sets on the market, and its linen sheets get top marks for that bedding category. Quince’s curtains and towels, too, have made us fans, and its velvet sectional received high praise from our audience development manager.

Given the brand’s well-established pattern of creating buy-worthy textiles and furniture, I was excited to test its newest fall bedding collection. As a quilt connoisseur, I opted to start with the European Linen Cotton Stitch Quilt Set—with shams, obviously. In light of the thermostat-swinging nature of the fall weather, I hoped the quilt set could function as a trans-seasonal solution and give my room a noticeable (and needed) refresh.

Quilts are often heavy, so I was surprised by how lightweight the package felt before I even unboxed it. As it turns out, the quilt is light by design, constructed from a combination of linen and cotton, with cotton fill that weighs just 200 grams per square meter (gsm)—or about the same as a flannel shirt by the square meter. I was relieved by its lack of heft, as my New York apartment’s heat runs on radiators that I can’t control and is, as a result, basically blazing hot at all times. Upon situating the quilt on my bed, I immediately searched the pick-stitching for loose threads. Devastatingly, I found some—a few pulled threads around the border—though not so exaggerated that I’d need to make my bed with caution.

The quilt set is framed by jade lamps and maroon shades to give surrounding color.

My experience with this quilt set was, in a word, peaceful. The best quilts offer a little warmth, a little weight, and you can comfortably pull them up and over you without struggling or yanking. All of that is possible with this Quince style. I like sleeping totally wrapped in this quilt. Because of the aforementioned radiator situation, it would be unbearably hot if I tried pairing this with a comforter, so I had only a flat sheet between myself and the quilt, which I found to be the perfect pairing. Its low-loft, pick-stitch construction and weight made it extremely easy to create a crisp bedscape. Its texture also disguises sheet wrinkles and lies super flat without much effort at all. I especially appreciate this quality in the shams, since my pillows usually look wrinkled and misshapen. And I do recommend opting for the bundle with the shams, which keep things looking put together.

After the quilt had a spin through the laundry, I noticed no differences in its soft feel. It came out of the dryer wrinkle-free, without any hint of being frayed or fried. And that’s saying something, as I battle-tested this quilt in a New York laundromat where it endured high heat and no option for a gentle cycle.

At $190 for a total bed refresh, this high-quality set is a nice buy. I love how it lies, and I believe it’ll be a big win for anyone who hates making their bed. The quilt set makes the center of my room look polished, but not stuffy. It skews a bit casual for my maximal taste, but I love the warm ivory option for a great guest-room spread, where I keep things more uniform and less lavish. Ultimately, the weight, the feel, and the price point make it one of the most solid quilt-set choices I’ve come across in my very diligent searching.

#5044. Bitcoin bounces from 7-week low under $76K as Democrats sweep Congress

Bitcoin price downside reached a seven-week-low of $75,618 as 24-hour crypto long liquidations neared $300 million.

Bitcoin (BTC) sought to erase 3% overnight losses on Wednesday as crypto markets digested the Democratic sweep of the midterm elections.

Data from TradingView showed BTC/USD falling into Tuesday’s daily close as election results came in, with Democrats going on to win control of both the House and Senate. The pair dropped to a low of $75,618 on Bitstamp, a level last seen on Sept. 16, before rebounding as high as $78,039 over the next three hours.

BTC/USD one-hour chart. Source: Cointelegraph/TradingView

Data from CoinGlass showed the price dip liquidating late BTC long positions below $76,000, while a wall of asks kept upside contained below $78,000. Total crypto long liquidations over the past 24 hours stood at $288 million.

BTC liquidation heatmap. Source: CoinGlass

In comments sent to Cointelegraph, Kelly Ye, head of research at institutional digital asset trading platform Talos, said that markets had largely priced in the election outcome.

“Bitcoin moved about 1.7% after the polls closed, close to the roughly 1.8% options had priced, and there was no major leverage flush. The election was the main event, but markets had largely prepared for it,” she said.

US stock futures showed similar moves, initially falling before recouping some losses. S&P 500 and Nasdaq 100 futures were both down by about 0.3% at the time of writing.

S&P 500 futures one-hour chart. Source: Cointelegraph/TradingView

Late Tuesday, Anthony Saglimbene, chief market strategist at financial services company Ameriprise Financial, told CNBC that concerns over data-center and broader AI capital expenditure were producing an adverse market reaction to the Democratic gains.

“If you get some governorships that are Democratic, what does that mean for the data center buildout, which has already seen a lot of skepticism over the last couple weeks in terms of the hyperscaler capex spending?” he asked.

In an interview with The Wall Street Journal, US President Donald Trump, who previously said he could become “Mr. Veto” to block Democratic policy initiatives, struck a conciliatory tone.

“I think we’ll work with the Democrats, and we’ll get along very well with them,” he said.

Bitcoin’s drop to $75,618 coincided with its 50-week simple moving average (SMA), a trend line that has held as support since mid-September.

Related: Here’s what happened in crypto today

In an analysis posted on X, Keith Alan, co-founder of crypto trading suite Material Indicators, identified the 50-week SMA as a line in the sand for Bitcoin bulls. Failure to defend it, he argued, would open the door to a “deeper correction ahead.”

BTC/USD one-day chart with 50 SMA. Source: Cointelegraph/TradingView

Crypto trader and analyst Rekt Capital, who had forecast BTC/USD dipping below $76,000 after a failed breakout attempt above $78,000, continued to flag overhead resistance on the three-day chart.

“If this continues then these newfound 3-Day Lower Highs may guide price back towards the ~$75.5k level below over time,” he said on Wednesday.

BTC/USD three-day chart. Source: Rekt Capital on X.com

Fleece is a soft, synthetic fabric usually made from a polyester blend, often mixed with spandex, nylon, or cotton. Designed to mimic wool's insulating properties, fleece is lighter, more breathable, and dries faster, making it ideal for staying warm. Plus, fleece wicks away moisture and dries quickly, keeping you cozy even if you’re caught in rain or snow. Think of it like wearing a warm hug on a cold day.

When it comes to fleece leggings, the fabric blend is key. Most leggings are made from a mix of polyester and spandex, which delivers the perfect blend of warmth, stretch, and comfort. We’ll give you a quick breakdown of why these materials are key here:

Polyester traps heat, making it an ideal blend for staying warm without bulk. Compared to natural fibers like cotton, polyester dries quickly. WH editors love wearing polyester-fleece leggings on long walks since they stay warm without making you feel sweaty. It’s super lightweight yet strong, meaning you won’t feel like the material is clinging to your body. Polyester also helps leggings retain their shape over time, so stretching, sagging, and pilling are likely a thing of the past.

Nylon is a popular material in fleece leggings because of its strength, flexibility, and smooth texture. It resists wear and tear, which helps keep leggings looking brand new after every wash. Nylon also offers water resistance, which is key for outdoor activities in light rain or snow.

Spandex (also known as elastane or Lycra) is the go-to fabric for stretch and flexibility. It fits snugly and moves with you, giving you maximum comfort during high-impact activities like yoga, running, and dance. While it’s not as durable on its own, spandex is often blended with other fabrics (like nylon or polyester) to create leggings that are both stretchy and long-lasting.

Here’s the thing: Fleece leggings with thicker fabric may feel more restrictive than traditional leggings, so if you plan to wear them for high-impact activities, opting for a bigger size might be best. Look for four-way stretch fabrics like spandex for flexibility and comfort. If you're between sizes, choose based on your desired fit—size down for a sculpted look (read: tighter) or up for a relaxed, lounge-friendly feel.

Fleece leggings are perfect for workouts, offering warmth and insulation while keeping the stretch and comfort you need for full range of motion. “The leggings should have a good amount of stretch while fitting snugly, and maintaining their shape throughout the day,” says fashion designer Kadeem Alphanso Fyffe. Look for features like a wide, high-rise waistband for a secure fit.

As writers who've tried more than our fair share of leggings, we know what it takes to make a great pair. To earn a place on this list, these fleece leggings had to meet strict criteria. We analyzed everything from fabric quality and warmth to stretch, fit, and breathability. Were they soft and supportive? Did they hold their shape after multiple washes? Could they withstand the elements without pilling or wearing thin? We examined every seam, ensuring each recommendation is a worthy investment for your winter wardrobe and beyond.

We also consulted two style experts, who shared their favorite pairs and provided insight on what to consider when shopping for fleece leggings. They emphasized that materials, stretch, and overall style matter, since they can make or break a pair of leggings (and pants, for that matter).

#5040. USDC Issuer Circle Quietly Cuts 5% of Staff in Profitable Quarter

USDC Issuer Circle Quietly Cuts 5% of Staff in Profitable Quarter

Circle, the company behind the USDC stablecoin, has cut about 5% of its workforce, according to its third-quarter earnings release on Wednesday.

The reduction was "designed to better align our organizational structure with our operating model and strategic priorities," the company said in footnotes to its financial tables. Circle did not say how many employees were affected or which teams were cut.

It recorded $9.1 million in restructuring expenses in the quarter, mostly cash payments for notice periods and severance, employee benefits and related costs. Those were partly offset by a $5.7 million reversal of stock-based compensation, as unvested shares held by departing employees were forfeited.

The cuts came in a profitable quarter. Circle reported net income from continuing operations of $226 million, up 5% from a year earlier, on total revenue and reserve income of $865 million, up 17%. Adjusted EBITDA jumped 85% year-on-year to $317 million.

Much of that growth came from outside Circle's core business. Other revenue rose to $207 million, including $182 million from the presale of ARC, the token for its Arc blockchain. Reserve income, the interest Circle earns on the assets backing USDC and its largest source of revenue, fell 8% to $658 million as interest rates on those reserves declined.

The average amount of USDC in circulation rose 8% from a year earlier to $73.3 billion, but ended the quarter flat at $73.9 billion. USDC's share of the U.S. dollar stablecoin market slipped to 27%, according to the company.

CEO Jeremy Allaire did not mention the job cuts in his comments on the results, or in a tweet on Wednesday calling Q3 "a big quarter for Circle." He said USDC carried 79% of all stablecoin transaction volume, up from 66% a year earlier, and that Circle's payments network ended the quarter at $41 billion in annualized volume, up 15-fold. "In January, the GENIUS Act takes effect and regulated digital dollars go mainstream," Allaire wrote. "Every layer of our platform is live and ready for it."

→ USDC carried 79% of all stablecoin transaction volume, up from 66% a year ago, and $13.9T moved onchain (+49%)

→ A record $208B of USDC was minted and redeemed, about $2.3B every day

→ Arc mainnet went live, with 100+ institutions and apps… pic.twitter.com/euVfyHBUXX

— Jeremy Allaire (@jerallaire) November 4, 2026

The cuts come as Circle expands. In September, it launched the public mainnet of its Arc blockchain, with BlackRock, DTCC and Visa among its permissioned validators. Allaire said in October that Circle plans to enter about 40 emerging and developing markets, and the company has since teamed up with payments-technology firm ACI Worldwide to pilot USDC settlement on Colombia's instant-payments network.

Circle is the latest in a string of crypto companies to cut staff this year, citing weaker markets, artificial intelligence or both.

Coinbase cut 14% of its workforce in May, with CEO Brian Armstrong pointing to the crypto downturn and the company's adoption of AI. The Ethereum Foundation cut 20% of its staff in June as part of a reorganization, while custody firm BitGo cut 15% that month to focus on AI and stablecoins.

Exchange Luno cut a fifth of its global staff in July, citing automation, and crypto ETF issuer Bitwise cut 14% of its staff in August.

#5039. Democrats win US House to secure control of Congress in midterms

Democrats will take control of House committees in January 2027, giving them power to investigate President Donald Trump’s administration.

Update (Nov. 4, 2026, 11:24 am UTC): This article has been updated to include comments from CertiK’s Stefan Muehlbauer on the election results and the outlook for US crypto legislation.

Democrats have won control of both chambers of the US Congress in the midterm elections, giving the party greater influence over legislation and oversight of President Donald Trump’s administration.

The party won House seats in districts where Trump beat his Democratic opponent by as much as 18 percentage points in 2024. Democrats also flipped at least seven Senate seats in Kansas, Iowa, Texas, Ohio, Maine, North Carolina and South Carolina, according to the Associated Press.

The result could reshape the outlook for crypto legislation and bring greater congressional scrutiny of Trump-linked digital asset businesses. Democratic committee leaders will gain more influence over which bills advance and which industry activities face investigation.

Stefan Muehlbauer, head of US government affairs at CertiK, said the results could derail the CLARITY crypto market structure bill.

“The immediate read on this election result is that CLARITY bill is likely dead,” he said in comments shared with Cointelegraph, referring to both the upcoming lame-duck session and the next Congress.

Muehlbauer said he expected Sen. Elizabeth Warren and Rep. Maxine Waters, both outspoken crypto critics, to chair the Senate Banking Committee and House Financial Services Committee, respectively.

He also said Democrats had fallen short of the Senate’s 60-vote threshold for overcoming a filibuster, limiting their ability to advance legislation.

Democrats will take control of House committees in January 2027, allowing them to issue subpoenas and investigate the Trump administration, the president’s family and their business interests, according to NBC News.

Financial markets commentary publication The Kobeissi Letter pointed to affordability as the defining election issue, citing high energy costs and renewed interest rate increases.

Gokhshtein Media founder David Gokhshtein said Republicans had performed worse than he expected.

“Well, Republicans got their asses handed to them. I thought they’d do better. I was wrong,” he wrote on X Wednesday.

Prediction markets had favored a Democratic sweep. Traders on Kalshi and Polymarket put the probability of Democrats winning both chambers at roughly 75% on Tuesday, before the results were announced.

#5038. 7 Best Throw Blanket Picks for Cozy Nights, Tested by AD (2026)

With cool evenings underway, and a list of books to keep me sane throughout winter, I’ve done my due diligence of putting together a comprehensive throw blanket list for a cozy night in. I’ve rounded up my personal favorites and asked other Shop AD writers which ones they love for movie marathons, napping, and working from home.

Our top picks are made from quality materials like Grade-A Mongolian cashmere and merino wool that are smooth and soft without feeling too heavy for lounging. The design factor was just as important in our research. As much as I love a neutral blanket that can blend in any background, these blankets come in a range of colors and patterns to fit different interior styles—some even created by design icons like Alexander Girard. I’ve prioritized machine-washable options, with a couple of worthy exceptions, and included care instructions so you know what to expect on laundry day. Browse all of the best blankets below.

This throw’s “Matisse” name is fitting because it’s a true masterpiece. The wool-blend blanket has made the rounds across the Shop AD team’s desks all year since it’s just as vibrant as it is thoughtfully made. It’s designed by Ezcaray, a Spain-based, family-owned and -operated textile house, known for artisan-made pieces like this one that are hand-washed, -dyed, and -woven. I had it for a while at my desk and can confirm that the “prairie sunrise” blanket’s colors look rich like pictured online. I wouldn’t consider myself a maximalist by any means, but I do appreciate the “more is more” color combinations and how easy it would be to style this in an eclectic bedroom filled with collected decor. In terms of texture, I’d say it almost feels like a goldendoodle’s fur. The fabric is soft like you’d expect, but it almost has a silkiness to it, likely because of the mohair added in the blend. The best part is that it has held up beautifully all year long with no signs of shedding.

If you want a blanket that’s soft without feeling weighty, you can’t go wrong with a good cashmere throw. I always look out for Grade-A Mongolian cashmere in particular since its fine, long fibers are more durable than cheaper cashmeres, like Grade-C, or wool blends. My colleague Lauren Arzbaecher, Shop AD shopping producer, put Quince’s version on my radar last year after talking about it so enthusiastically at the office. She told me it felt “insanely soft” and always kept her comfortably “bundled up while reading a good book.”

My take? Everything Arzbaecher told me was true plus more. It feels breathable enough to keep me warm without sweating on the nights my apartment radiator is cranked up too high. I have the blanket in a deep moss color that fits my moody home design, but there are a dozen other colors, so you can find what works best for your space.

Even though I’ve tried my fair share of soft throw blankets in my life, this one is a favorite since it hits a trifecta of sorts: It’s incredibly fluffy, warm, and easy to care for. It’s made of 100% faux mohair, but Pom Pom at Home has me fooled. The blanket has a brushed finish that gives the blanket an ultrasoft feel, so much so that it’s quickly become my cold-weather companion this year. Ever since the temperature dipped below 50 degrees, I’ve kept it draped on my sofa for countless naps and cozy movie nights.

Fair warning: I’m quite gentle with this blanket, but I could see it easily snagging on sharp furniture corners if you’re not mindful. I haven’t noticed general material shedding in the last couple of months I’ve had the blanket, so that’s a good sign for longevity, especially since it’s a fuzzier-looking fabric.

Of course, I’d prefer it if it were made of pure mohair, but the fact that it resembles the material without looking or feeling cheap is a huge win. I’d recommend this piece to anyone who wants a plushy blanket without paying a steep price.

Architect and interior designer Alexander Girard wore a few hats during his career, one of them being a textile designer for Braniff International Airways. He designed this blanket as part of a 17,543-item brand-identity system for the airline, known for its bold design, in 1965. Today, Pendleton re-creates the iconic multi-stripe design, and we think it’s a great choice for anyone looking for a personality piece. “As a design enthusiast, no surface of mine goes unadorned—even a lowly desk chair deserves a decorative blanket, especially in a chilly office like Condé Nast’s with the AC blasting,” says Arzbaecher. “This striped wool throw rests over the back of my office chair, providing extra cushion and much-needed color.”

The design is understandably a huge draw, but its texture deserves just as much attention. It’s a smooth wool blend (82% wool and 18% cotton), and Arzbaecher does not find it the least bit “scratchy or too thick to the touch.”

Contributor Yelena Alpert considers this her travel blanket—it moves from her bed to the family room couch, and everyone fights over who gets to curl up under it. “The grand waffle texture is certainly noteworthy, but it is the warmth factor that makes this throw a winner,” she says, adding that it feels like her favorite cable-knit sweater. “Since it is made from merino wool, your body is immediately ensconced as if in a hug. It does not feel scratchy or itchy, in case you have a fear of wool.” The knotted fringe at the end of the throw might seem like a minimal detail, but Alpert says it makes her sectional look even cozier to snuggle up on.

“I am also a huge fan of the camel color,” she says. “It pairs so beautifully with my plum matelassé duvet cover, but also enriches the rosewood hue of my velvet quilt.”

My pretty-in-pink throw blanket has never failed to put a smile on my face in the last year. Its salmon color is vibrant and warm without going full Barbiecore. It’s also the most detailed herringbone blanket I’ve ever had—for those who are new to this design too, the pattern has a distinct V shape that looks like the skeleton of a herring fish. Sadly, some of the little loops have snagged on my watch as I’ve unwrapped myself to get up for a lunch break, so it’s something to think about if you’re worried about a pet clawing at your blanket.

The throw is another mainstay at my office desk when I need to thaw out after a cold subway commute, and it hasn’t disappointed. I’ve even lent it to my coworkers who could use an extra layer during the day, and they say it’s warm without feeling stuffy.

This is technically a bed blanket, so the size ranges from a Twin to a California King, but it doesn’t have the typical bulk of a big bedspread. Alpert likes the blanket since it’s lightweight and folds pretty flat when she places it at the foot of her bed. “I love the barely-there feel of the blanket and use it almost every night as I read stories to my son,” she says. “It provides just enough cover to keep me comfortable without putting me to sleep.” It also features thoughtful, grid-like stitching that gives her whole sleep setup a little extra character.

In true Brooklinen fashion, there are a few distinct hues to choose from, including muted and vibrant colors. “I have this in the storm [colorway], a somewhat dusty blue—and as the name would suggest, it looks like the sky before it rains,” Alpert says. “It almost works like a neutral and pairs well against a crisp white sheet—I use it with a percale cotton set, which has swirls of blue. I also use it with my linen sheets, and it creates this decadent, heirloom-quality bed.”

#5036. Binance hackers move 81% of stolen Solana, route funds via THORChain

The Binance attackers moved most of the stolen SOL and continued laundering funds through THORChain after the protocol resumed swaps.

The attackers behind the $6.3 billion Binance hack have moved about 81% of the stolen Solana (SOL) as they continue laundering assets through the cross-chain swap protocol THORChain.

Wallets grouped under the Binance hacker entity held 182,443 SOL (worth $16.9 million), or roughly 18.8% of the total 970,522 SOL stolen during the exploit, according to blockchain data platform Arkham.

Lookonchain reported on Wednesday that the attackers split SOL across multiple wallets, selling it for USDC, bridging to Ethereum to buy Ether, and ultimately swapping ETH for Bitcoin (BTC) via THORChain.

Binance suffered a record-setting hack on Thursday after attackers compromised employee devices through fake job interviews and malicious software downloads, the exchange said. Binance said Tuesday it replenished 98% of affected tokens and would absorb losses.

The US Federal Bureau of Investigation attributed the attack to North Korea’s TraderTraitor hackers on Saturday and urged crypto service providers, including THORNode operators, to block transactions linked to stolen assets.

Kim Yo Jong, sister of North Korean leader Kim Jong Un, dismissed the accusations as “absurd rhetoric.”

North Korea-linked hacking groups are suspected of using THORChain to launder proceeds from some of the largest cryptocurrency hacks, including the $1.4 billion Bybit hack.

THORChain restored trading on Bitcoin, Ethereum and several other chains on Wednesday. Services had been halted on Saturday after an Ethereum block reorganization exposed a refund bug. The halt inadvertently delayed Binance hackers’ laundering activity, according to Chainalysis.

Blockchain investigator SomaXBT estimated that about 54% of the attackers’ more than 47,600 transactions on Tron and Solana were automated.

Binance hackers expanded their laundering activity to Solana on Sunday after emptying most of their temporary holding wallets on Tron. Chainalysis estimated that about $500 million in total value moved through Tron, including repeated transfers between wallets.

Related: Binance’s net inflows turn positive at $2.3B as withdrawals expand

#5035. What Olympic Gold Medalist Tara Davis-Woodhall Uses to Recover After Her Hardest Training Days

The workout gear and personal must-haves that help her feel ready anywhere.

Women's Health may earn commission from the links on this page, but we only feature products we believe in. Why Trust Us?

Winner of a gold medal at the 2024 Summer Olympics. Winner of the silver medal in the 2023 World Championships. Coach at Kansas State University. These are just a few ways to describe the track and field phenom Tara Davis-Woodhall.

Women’s Health recently got the chance to pick Davis-Woodhall’s brain about her fitness gear essentials—the training and recovery tools she can’t live without. Consider this her list of training must-haves. And some of her picks might surprise you (hint: one of them smells really good).

These slides with built-in heat and vibration have become Davis-Woodhall’s favorite way to recover after training (our feet need recovery, too!) “The heat and vibration feel incredible after long days, and they’re so comfortable that I honestly don't want to take them off,” Davis-Woodhall tells Women’s Health.

She slips them on after practice, when she’s traveling, and whenever it feels like her feet need a little extra TLC. One of the reasons these slides have become her go-to is that since they’re footwear, she can still multitask while her feet are getting the recovery benefits of heat and vibration therapy. “I love that I can recover while still moving around and winding down after a long day,” she says.

While journaling may not be the first thing that pops into your mind when you think “fitness essentials of a pro athlete,” Davis-Woodhall considers it an essential training tool. “My journal is always with me because it’s where I can slow down, reset, and get my thoughts out of my head,” she tells WH. “I use it to write down goals, reflect on training and competitions, and keep track of the little things that are working for me.”

Training isn’t the only thing Davis-Woodhall examines and processes during her journaling, which she says has become a really important part of her routine. “Sometimes it’s about track & field, but sometimes I also just need a moment to write and reset, “ she says.

Hydration is important for all of us, but even more so for athletes performing at the highest level. “Hydration is a huge part of my routine,” Davis-Woodhall says. She has additional dehydrating demands on her body outside of training like extensive traveling and spending a lot of time training outdoors.

While there are many electrolyte supplements on the market, Davis-Woodhall has found her favorite in Cadence. It helps her stay consistent because she enjoys the taste, especially the Melonberry and Orange Soda flavors. “I drink it before, during, and after training to help replace the electrolytes I'm losing and stay hydrated. I also love that it has zero sugar and no caffeine, so I can drink it throughout the day.”

Last year, a study examining the effects of athletes using music to boost mood, and that that mood boost can in turn have a positive impact on physical performance. Davis-Woodhall is no stranger to using music as a tool in her training. “I never go anywhere without my headphones,” she says. “Whether I’m warming up, cooling down, traveling, or just need a little time in my own world, music is such a big part of how I get into the right mindset.”

But getting hyped and ready isn’t the only time Davis-Woodhall taps into music to help her maintain peak performance. Sometimes she uses her headphones to tune out distractions and reset.

We love the Bose QuietComfort Ultra Bluetooth Headphones, our pick for best noise cancelling headphones, for doing just that.

Music isn’t the only thing that can get you in the right frame of mind. A signature scent can, too. “I love a good fragrance, and BOSS Bottled Beyond is one I always have with me,” Davis-Woodhall says. “I love the scent, but I also love what it represents: feeling confident, powerful, and lifting up the women around you.”

Davis-Woodhall wears this fragrance when she’s getting ready for competition but also reaches for it in her day-to-day life. “It's one of those little things that makes me feel put together and ready for whatever I'm walking into,” she says.

To help keep her legs fresh and ready, Davis-Woodhall knows that supporting healthy leg circulation is crucial. A device she uses to help with this is Firefly, a wireless band that wraps around the top of the calf and emits stimulating pulses that mimic the muscle contractions of walking, which helps increase circulation. “Between training, competing, and constantly traveling, recovery is such a huge part of what I do. I love Firefly because it’s small enough to keep in my track bag, so I can use it right after training,” she says, noting that it’s become a tool she relies on all season.

The best recovery tools that we use in our routines are sometimes the simplest. Case in point: the yoga mat and massage ball Davis-Woodhall keeps on hand. “I always have these around for stretching, mobility, and working through any tight spots after training or in the middle of a competition. I use the roller ball a lot on my feet and legs, and the yoga mat gives me a place to go through my normal stretching and mobility routine.”

The Manuka Pro yoga mat is one that WH editors love for its extra thickness and no-slip surface, and the Rad Rounds set of three different sizes of massage balls with different densities will cover any spot you’re trying to release.

WH editors love compression boots, and so does Davis-Woodhall, particularly the Hyperice Normatec Elite Legs. “This is my go-to after my hardest training days, especially after long sprint days since I started hurdling again this season,” she says. “My legs can definitely feel heavy after those workouts, so I usually put them on in the evening while Hunter and I are relaxing or watching TV.” While Davis-Woodhall uses these compression boots throughout her season, she is especially consistent leading into a competition to make sure her legs feel fresh and ready to jump.

Natascha is an editor and writer covering health, fitness, and the ways people move through the world. She’s been a NASM-certified personal trainer for more than a decade, with a background in functional strength training, corrective exercise, nutrition, and behavior change. She has additional CES, CNC, BCS, and SFC certifications. She is also a level-2 certified Spinning instructor. Her work has appeared in Bicycling, Runner’s World, and other outlets, and her experience spans both editorial and hands-on work across the fitness industry. Whether she’s writing about training, health, or performance, she’s most interested in making complicated topics useful, approachable, and helpful. She is currently the fitness and reviews editor for Women’s Health.

#5034. 11 Christmas Centerpiece Ideas That Are Fa-la-la-la-bulous

A festive Christmas centerpiece is not just a decorative focal point, it also sets the scene for making memories around the table. Whether you dream up a candy cane–inspired color scheme with elegant white china and poinsettia-red floral arrangements, or you create a winter tablescape complete with snowflakes, snowmen, and flickering tea lights, a holiday table is the perfect opportunity to get creative and channel your yuletide style. And the good news is, a holiday centerpiece isn’t just for a dining table—kitchen islands, foyers, and coffee tables all lend themselves well to a festive display. To inspire you, we’ve gathered our favorite Christmas centerpiece ideas that include everything from a classic cranberry-red palette to a designer’s chic jewel-toned Christmas table.

If you’re going for understated elegance, take cues from this Christmas table. Layer together natural elements like foraged foliage, evergreen branches, pinecones, and antlers, then add height with candleholders. “The palette is deliberately restrained and a departure from traditional holiday decor to maintain elegance and elevate the materials,” says Rae McCalmont of PBF Homes. The Christmas decor leans into deep forest greens, warm walnut tones, soft ivory, and earthy neutrals. “Layered greenery spills organically across the table to keep the presentation soft and unfussy, metallic hints, a mix of etched and colored glassware, and eclectic dinnerware elevate the presentation, while softly glowing candlelight introduces warmth,” McCalmont says.

“When planning for my Christmas dining table centerpiece, I found these fabulous candlesticks in amber, peach, and gold to complement my dining room wall mural without screaming ‘red and green’ like the typical holiday colors,” says Maggie Griffin, founder and lead designer at Maggie Griffin Design. She arranged the collection of candlesticks with white tapers down the center of the table, while “touches of jewel tones in the glassware and table linens add to the festive vibe and layered tabletop,” Griffin shares.

Pack a punch with a large Christmas arrangement that adds a festive pop of color. When assembling a seasonal DIY centerpiece, consider the vessel as an integral part of the arrangement. Here, Griffin layered seasonal magnolia and bright red berries in deep coral and celadon jars that she keeps on the kitchen counter year-round. “It’s a nod to the holiday season without the more traditional color scheme,” she says. “I believe in enhancing your home for the holidays, not totally redecorating it,” Griffin adds.

“We wanted the holiday decor to feel like a natural extension of the room rather than introduce an entirely new Christmas palette,” says Lindsey Herod, owner and principal designer at Lindsey Herod Interiors. The designer believes a room’s color story is a great starting point for any holiday table centerpiece. “Here, the centerpiece and holiday greenery can be lush and romantic because they’re speaking the same language as the chintz, antiques, and existing palette; it feels festive, but still completely part of the house,” Herod explains.

“It can be fun to look beyond traditional holiday themes and embrace decor that evokes the sense of magic and wonder that the season is all about,” says interior designer Bethany Adams, founder of Bethany Adams Interiors. For this centerpiece inspired by her late grandmother’s favorite china pattern, Adams wanted to create a quiet woodland that the deer on the mantel might have just visited. “A soft bed of moss, mushrooms of various sizes, and elegant taper candles lead the eye toward the mantel and its exuberant display of greenery,” she says. The best part? “Without obvious holiday themes, this centerpiece works all the way through the New Year,” Adams says.

Add a festive touch to a holiday party table or kitchen island with a custom gingerbread house. “We designed the gingerbread house as a playful nod to the home’s architecture, complete with an illuminated interior that echoes its expansive front entry windows,” says Kristi Will, founder of Kristi Will Interior Design. To make it extra special, the gingerbread house was personalized with the family’s dogs at the front door to welcome guests. “Created as the centerpiece for their holiday party, it brought a sense of whimsy and personality to the heart of the home,” Will says.

Embrace Christmas nostalgia by turning a vintage tabletop tree into a charming holiday centerpiece. Interior designer Bambi A’Lynn Bratton, founder of Bambi A’Lynn Interior Design, has a white Christmas tree, so this vintage German tree was the ideal choice for her table. “I have collected antique German ornaments for years and needed a special place to display them, so when I came across this little tree I knew it was the perfect fit,” she says. “I have also used this piece on a coffee table when I have a dinner party during the holidays, or on the hearth of a fireplace on top of a stack of books,” the designer shares.

For an elegant holiday table, you can’t go wrong with floral arrangements in classic Christmas red. “When it comes to styling the floral arrangements, I always make sure the florals are low enough so everyone at the table can see each other easily, but still bountiful enough so everyone can admire the gorgeous flowers used,” says interior stylist Carolyne Steiner of The Carolyne Collective. To complete the holiday tablescape, Steiner dotted white candlesticks in between the florals and placed ornaments on the plates. “I collect ornaments throughout the year for each guest invited to my event—it serves as their place card,” she shares. “Before we sit down for our meal, each person has to guess which place setting is theirs; it ends up becoming a really fun game, conversation piece, and icebreaker before dinner begins,” Steiner says.

Use a potted white orchid as an elegant Christmas centerpiece on a foyer table for a festive welcome. “The white orchid is just a lovely neutral decor item that adds some formality to a space,” says Grace Brackman, founder and principal designer at Grace Brackman Design. She notes that white orchids look particularly beautiful paired with more traditional decor. “Add some interest by potting it in a pretty ceramic bowl enhanced with some fresh-cut magnolia and fir,” Brackman suggests.

When creating a Christmas centerpiece, think beyond the surface of the dining table. “Decorating the chandelier draws the eye upward and makes the entire table feel more festive, rather than limiting the holiday elements to the tablescape itself,” says Lauren Sullivan, founder and curator at Well x Design. Here, red ribbon on the chandelier echoes the ribbon on the Christmas wreaths that hang from each chair, and visually extends the centerpiece beyond the table. “It also helps connect the table to the rest of the room and makes the whole setting feel more complete,” Sullivan says.

Arrange mercury glass trees and votive holders down the center of the dinner table to add height and create a magical ambience. “The reflective qualities of mercury glass make it a great choice for a candlelight dinner—it sets the mood and makes it feel extra festive,” Brackman says. To create an organic table runner and a soft backdrop of color for the trees to sit on, the designer recommends piling fresh-cut greenery at the base of the mercury pieces. “In my opinion, live greenery is a necessity as nothing beats the smell of fresh-cut pine,” Brackman states.

#5033. US midterm elections 2026 as it happened: Democrats win control of Congress

Democrats won control of Congress in the 2026 US midterms. Catch up on key races, crypto-backed candidates and the outlook for crypto regulation.

Democrats have won control of both chambers of the US Congress, reshaping the outlook for cryptocurrency legislation and giving the party greater power to scrutinize President Donald Trump’s crypto ventures during the final two years of his term.

Live coverage has ended. Read our updates on key congressional races, crypto-backed candidates and election-night reactions below. Results and race calls are based on Associated Press reporting. Entries appear in reverse chronological order.

Republican Sen. Pete Ricketts has defeated independent challenger Dan Osborn in Nebraska, where crypto political action committee Fairshake had reportedly spent nearly $11 million supporting Ricketts.

More recent spending data shows Fairshake-affiliated crypto super PAC Defend American Jobs spent approximately $13.5 million opposing Osborn, according to Federal Election Commission data compiled by Bankrolled as of Nov. 3.

Ricketts, a member of the Senate Banking Committee, has supported cryptocurrency legislation including the CLARITY Act and GENIUS Act. He voted to advance the CLARITY Act out of committee in May and supported an unsuccessful Senate procedural vote on the legislation in September, according to crypto advocacy group Stand With Crypto.

Democrats have won control of the US House of Representatives, according to the Associated Press, flipping a chamber Republicans have held since 2023. Rep. Hakeem Jeffries is in line to become Speaker.

Control of the House gives Democrats the committee chairs and subpoena power. NBC says that includes oversight of Trump’s family and business ventures.

Democrat Troy Jackson defeated Republican Sen. Susan Collins in Maine, according to the Associated Press.

Collins was one of four Republicans to oppose advancing the CLARITY Act in September, expressing concerns over its impact on bank deposits.

Unlike Collins, Jackson has expressed support for clear cryptocurrency legislation in the run-up to the election. According to Stand with Crypto, which rates Jackson as “very pro crypto,” he called to end the debanking of “lawful crypto businesses, and ensure Americans’ right to self-custody digital assets.”

“My message is congratulations to the Democrats, and I look forward to working with you for the good of our country,” President Donald Trump told CBS News’ senior White House correspondent Weijia Jiang.

Democrats have won control of the US Senate, according to the Associated Press.

The majority was secured after Democrat Josh Turek won the Senate race in Iowa, bringing Democrats and independents who caucus with them to 51 seats.

The result reshapes the outlook for the stalled CLARITY Act and determines which lawmakers lead committees overseeing the industry.

Democratic control could also bring more congressional scrutiny of Trump-linked crypto businesses and regulators’ decisions. Control of the Senate could also affect confirmation of future presidential nominees to regulatory posts.

“It’s too early to say exactly what this means for crypto, but one thing is clear: nothing will pass without cross-partisan support,” Jerome de Tychey, president of Ethereum France and co-founder of EthCC, told Cointelegraph.

Democrats are now in control of 50 Senate seats — one short of a majority — after flipping five seats in GOP states, some that hadn’t seen a Democratic senator in power for decades.

Earlier, Decision Desk HQ projected the Democratic Party to win a majority in the US House of Representatives.

In Ohio, Democrat Sherrod Brown, a longtime crypto critic, beat incumbent Republican Senator Jon Husted in a race that has attracted approximately $63 million in crypto industry spending.

That includes about $56 million from Fairshake and $7 million from the Winklevoss-backed Digital Freedom Fund, with both groups spending to support Husted or oppose Brown.

Crypto advocacy group Stand With Crypto characterizes Brown as strongly opposed to cryptocurrency, citing 17 statements and his 2024 vote against overturning the SEC’s SAB 121 crypto custody accounting guidance.

Brown was also targeted by crypto industry spending in 2024, when Fairshake affiliate Defend American Jobs spent more than $40 million supporting Republican Bernie Moreno, who defeated Brown in that year’s Senate election.

Democrat James Talarico has won Texas’ US Senate race against Republican Ken Paxton, according to the Associated Press, flipping another Republican-held seat.

Talarico becomes the first Democrat to win a statewide election in Texas since 1994. As of 11 pm ET, he led Paxton by approximately 1.09 million votes, with 56.1% to Paxton’s 42.2%, according to Decision Desk HQ. Counting was continuing.

Paxton received support from the Winklevoss-backed Digital Freedom Fund, although both candidates earned A ratings from crypto advocacy group Stand With Crypto.

Paxton has called for the passage of the CLARITY Act, arguing that clear regulations are necessary to maintain US competitiveness in digital assets. In a June statement, he said the country must become the “crypto capital of the world” and support innovation and growth.

Talarico has similarly called for clear regulations that protect consumers while allowing innovation. As a Texas state representative, he also voted in 2025 to establish a state strategic Bitcoin reserve, according to legislative records cited by Stand With Crypto.

Democrat Annie Andrews has defeated Republican Sen. Darline Graham in South Carolina, according to the Associated Press, giving Democrats another pickup.

Andrews’ victory ends more than two decades of Republican control of the seat. The last time South Carolina elected a Democrat to the Senate was in 1998.

Following their victory in North Carolina, Democrats would need two further Republican-held seats to reach a majority, provided they retain all their Democratic-held seats.

Decision Desk HQ has projected the Democratic Party to win a majority in the US House of Representatives.

Democrats and allied independents stood at 45 Senate seats as of 10:02 pm ET, six short of the 51 needed for a majority.

Their path includes retaining Democratic-held seats in Oregon, Michigan and Minnesota and flipping three additional Republican-held seats. Democrats have already picked up North Carolina.

NBC News has projected that Democrat Annie Andrews will defeat incumbent Republican Senator Darline Graham in South Carolina in a major upset. Associated Press has not yet called this race.

Republican Representative Andy Barr has won Kentucky’s US Senate race, sending a supporter of the CLARITY and GENIUS Acts to the upper chamber.

Fairshake-affiliated political action committees spent more than $7 million supporting Barr in the Republican primary. Barr has also introduced legislation aimed at protecting lawful businesses, including crypto firms, from debanking.

Democrat Juliana Stratton has won Illinois’ US Senate race, succeeding retiring Democratic Sen. Dick Durbin.

Stratton advanced through the Democratic primary despite substantial opposition from crypto-backed PAC Fairshake, which had spent more than $5.5 million against her by early March.

Republican Representative Harriet Hageman has won Wyoming’s US Senate race against Democrat James Byrd, succeeding retiring crypto advocate Senator Cynthia Lummis.

Lummis, who chairs the Senate Banking Subcommittee on Digital Assets, sponsored the BITCOIN Act of 2025 to establish a US strategic Bitcoin reserve and has supported the CLARITY and GENIUS Acts.

Hageman has also backed cryptocurrency legislation, voting to pass the CLARITY and GENIUS Acts in 2025 and the FIT21 market structure bill in 2024. She also cosponsored legislation seeking to prohibit the Federal Reserve from issuing a central bank digital currency, according to Stand With Crypto. The advocacy group lists no documented position on cryptocurrency for Byrd.

Kalshi and Polymarket traders now give Democrats a 99% chance of winning Senate control and price House control at nearly 100%. It came as Democrats flipped North Carolina’s Senate seat, while holding Georgia and New Hampshire.

Prediction markets increasingly favored Democrats taking control of both chambers of Congress on Tuesday evening.

As of 8:05 pm ET, the Blockchain Association’s election dashboard showed Democratic Senate-control odds at 94% on Kalshi and 93% on Polymarket, up from 75% and 75.5%, respectively, earlier in the day. Democratic House-control odds stood at 98% and 99%.

At the time, Democrats and allied independents have secured 42 Senate seats to Republicans’ 31.

Massachusetts Senator Ed Markey and Delaware Senator Chris Coons, both Democrats, win reelection, according to the Associated Press.

Markey voted against the GENIUS stablecoin bill in 2025, while Coons has criticized President Donald Trump’s crypto ventures, calling his latest memecoin-holder event “corruption.”

Democratic Sen. Jon Ossoff has won reelection in Georgia, beating Republican Representative Mike Collins.

Ossoff expressed support for impeaching Trump in April 2025 over plans to host a private dinner for top holders of the president’s TRUMP memecoin, arguing that the arrangement amounted to selling access to the presidency.

Maylea Ma, deputy general counsel at 1inch, said she expects federal agencies to drive the next phase of crypto regulation.

The election could reshape the path forward for the stalled CLARITY Act, but Ma said “real movement” in US digital-asset regulation over the next few years will come from the US Securities and Exchange Commission, Commodity Futures Trading Commission and Treasury, rather than Congress.

Ma said those agencies were increasingly engaging with crypto projects. However, agency engagement does not provide the stability the industry had hoped to secure through legislation.

“The long-term goal, of course, remains a statute that cannot be reversed every time a government changes,” said Ma.

Democratic Rep. Debbie Wasserman Schultz wins Florida’s 20th Congressional District. Wasserman Schultz voted against the CLARITY crypto market structure bill when it passed the House in July 2025.

Democratic Senator Mark Warner has won reelection in Virginia, defeating Republican challenger Bert Mizusawa. Warner was widely favored to win.

Warner voted against advancing the CLARITY Act in September, arguing that crypto legislation needs safeguards against President Donald Trump personally benefiting from the industry. Warner supports establishing federal rules for digital assets, with his opposition to the bill focused on its ethics protections.

The first polls in the US midterm elections closed at 6 pm ET in parts of Indiana and Kentucky. The remaining polls in both states close at 7 pm ET.

Polls also close at 7 pm ET in Georgia and Virginia, followed by Ohio at 7:30 pm ET, where Republican Sen. Jon Husted faces Democrat Sherrod Brown in a race drawing major crypto industry spending.

More than 57 million Americans had cast ballots as of Monday, with early voting surpassing 2022 levels in several closely watched states, according to the Associated Press.

Current balance of power in the US House and Senate. Source: Blockchain Association

#5032. Bitcoin Shrugs Off Midterms as Experts Say Democratic Sweep Was 'Priced In'

Bitcoin Shrugs Off Midterms as Experts Say Democratic Sweep Was 'Priced In'

Democrats flipped both the House of Representatives and the Senate in the U.S. midterms, handing President Donald Trump a major political setback.

Experts say this was not the "preferred outcome" for crypto markets and that it has increased "uncertainty" for future pro-crypto policy. However, after a brief dip, Bitcoin is up 1.6% over the past 24 hours and now sits at $78,277, per Decrypt data. Broadly, almost every top 100 crypto token by market cap is in the green for the day.

"I think the Democratic sweep was largely priced in going into the midterms," André Dragosch, head of research at Bitwise Europe, told Decrypt. "The downside risks were fairly limited going into these midterms as sentiment was already very bearish and there were already signs of seller exhaustion again. So, it is no surprise that we have seen this kind of V-shaped recovery on an intraday basis during the midterms."

Polymarket put odds of the Democratic Party sweeping both the House and Senate during the midterms at 65.5% at the start of the week. Dragosch said these odds meant that markets weren't shocked by Tuesday's results.

James Butterfill, head of research at CoinShares, agreed. "My sense is that Bitcoin had largely priced in a Democratic sweep, leaving room for a relief rally as uncertainty clears," he said in comments shared with Decrypt. "Therefore, the anticipated result need not trigger another sell-off."

"Crypto policy did not decide the midterms. The economy, gas prices, the Iran situation, and Trump's unmet promises did," Heslin Kim, co-founder and CBO of Zenith, told Decrypt. "Crypto policy was irrelevant to the broader voting pool, but it now has the baggage of all the egregious activity that was stirred up in our ecosystem associated with the Trump family."

Tim Sun, senior analyst at HashKey, told Decrypt that the results do "increase the uncertainty surrounding the progress of crypto policy," but that Bitcoin's price action means that the market doesn't interpret it as a "major negative catalyst."

Specifically, this may be because the midterm results don't mean the Democrats can roll back existing pro-crypto guidelines set out by the SEC and CFTC, Dragosch said. However, it does mean that going forward, the market cannot rely as heavily on Trump's "political tailwinds for momentum," Sun added.

It also complicates the picture for the Clarity Act, which would establish rules for crypto markets. The bill has been in limbo since it was introduced in 2025, with many Democrats opposing it due to Trump's crypto conflicts of interest.

Dmitry Machikhin, CEO of BitOK, predicted in a note shared with Decrypt that the Clarity Act will remain in limbo well into 2027, thanks to the midterm results.

"The greater concern is slower crypto reform," Butterfill said. "CLARITY faces a harder path, while investigations into Trump-linked ventures could consume political capital and generate uncomfortable headlines across the sector. But the administration remains in place, and existing regulatory progress, including GENIUS, does not simply disappear."

He expects U.S. exchanges, token issuers and businesses that rely on new regulatory permissions to be more sensitive to the result than Bitcoin, and said established, compliant operators could even gain if scrutiny falls disproportionately on weaker competitors.

"A Democratic Congress may even be good for the industry in one way," Marcin Kaźmierczak, co-founder and COO of RedStone, told Decrypt. "Crypto shouldn't depend on one political camp. Infrastructure has to work for whoever is in power and bring most value to the ecosystem participants. Finance is changing, and politics will not stop it."

Despite the switch-up in the House and Senate, Dragosch expects little to change over the next two years.

"I personally expect more disputes, disagreements, and simply more of 'nothing stops this train,'" he said, pointing to a lack of significant progress to rein in fiscal deficits. "We will probably remain in a fiscally dominant environment with increasing fiscal risks, which I think is ultimately a tailwind for hard assets like Bitcoin."

Instead, he believes macro factors "remain in the driver's seat for now." Specifically, he points to a potential upcoming pivot from the Federal Reserve as a response to "increasing risk" around the AI trade.

"This will likely cause renewed interest in debasement-resistant assets like Bitcoin," Dragosch said, "and will continue to drive institutional adoption via ETPs and treasury companies as well."

Butterfill also pointed to macro factors. "For Bitcoin, real yields, dollar liquidity and ETP flows still carry more weight than the elections," he said. "I would temper expectations for the pace of U.S. reform, while remaining constructive on institutional adoption. How Trump responds to being constrained is the less predictable part."

#5028. Bitcoin ETFs lose $1.16B as Bitwise calls selling ‘short-term’

Bitwise’s André Dragosch attributed Bitcoin ETF outflows to weaker market sentiment, saying institutions have largely maintained their exposure.

US spot Bitcoin exchange-traded funds (ETFs) recorded more than $1 billion in net outflows over three trading days, but crypto asset manager Bitwise says the withdrawals do not signal a lasting retreat by institutional investors.

Investors pulled another $116 million from the funds on Tuesday, bringing net outflows since Friday to $1.16 billion, according to SoSoValue data.

“I do think it’s related to a short-term risk-off environment rather than a structural change in institutional sentiment,” Bitwise’s head of research, André Dragosch, told Cointelegraph.

The withdrawals followed Binance’s $6.3 billion hack on Thursday, which raised concerns about investor confidence and ETF custody security.

Dragosch attributed the outflows to weaker market sentiment rather than fears over the safety of ETF holdings.

Dragosch said ETF flows reflect both long-term institutional adoption and shorter-term changes in investor sentiment.

He cited a recent Bitwise survey of 15 institutions, none of which had reduced their Bitcoin ETF exposure during the bear market. US regulatory filings also showed that most institutional investor categories, apart from hedge funds, had largely maintained their positions.

Related: Bitcoin ETF inflows leave institutional demand unclear: CoinShares

Retail investors still account for the majority of Bitcoin ETF holdings and tend to react more strongly to market volatility, according to Dragosch.

Bitwise’s analysis found that ETF inflows are closely tracked broader crypto market sentiment, which weakened around the Binance hack, he said.

Tuesday’s $116 million in outflows marked a slowdown from Monday’s $399 million and Friday’s $643 million, extending the funds’ withdrawal streak to three trading sessions.

The Crypto Fear & Greed Index rose to 34 from 26 on Monday, though it remained in the “Fear” category. Bitcoin gained about 1.6% over the past 24 hours, to trade near $78,000 at publication, according to CoinGecko.

The latest withdrawals brought the funds’ net outflows for 2026 to approximately $153 million.

Related: Binance traces $6.3B hack to fake job interviews, compromised AI tool

The Binance hack has raised concerns among some industry observers about crypto ETFs custody, particularly because major funds rely on a small number of custodians.

Dragosch pointed to differences between the security arrangements used by crypto exchanges and institutional custodians, noting that the Binance incident affected hot wallets, which are connected to the internet, rather than cold wallets.

“In contrast, institutional funds held at Coinbase custody are custodied in cold storage which, by definition, are not connected to the internet,” Dragosch said.
